========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Uh, yeah… I guess the short version is work kind of pushed me to come in. I keep forgetting things I’m supposed to do—scheduled callbacks, work orders, basic follow-ups I told people I’d handle. And then I’ll remember later, like way too late, and by then it’s already caused a problem.

It’s gotten piled up enough that they said I need to get it checked out before I mess up my chance to move up. Which is frustrating, because I know the job, I’ve been doing this kind of work a long time… it’s not that I don’t care. I just keep losing track of things I swore I’d remember.

Yeah… focusing is definitely part of it. Like I’ll open a digital work order thinking, okay, I’m going to handle this, I’ve got the notes right here. I just keep losing track of things I swore I’d remember.\n\nDoctor: Are you also forgetting things outside of work, like appointments or tasks you need to do at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the big thing, the thing that got me sent here, is more at work. I keep forgetting scheduled callbacks and work orders, and even just basic follow-ups I told people I’d take care of. Like I’ll promise someone, “Yeah, I’ve got it,” and then it just… disappears on me until it’s already become a problem.\n\nAt home, I don’t know, it’s harder to separate out because everybody forgets stuff, right? Like you set something down and then—anyway. But what really piled up and got serious is the work stuff. They basically told me I need to get checked out before I ruin my chance at moving up.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble remembering things like birthdays, anniversaries, or personal appointments outside of work?\n\nPatient: I mean… birthdays and anniversaries, I’m not really sure that’s the main thing. I have calendars and people remind me, you know? What actually brought me in is more like at work, where I’ll have a scheduled callback or a work order, or I’ll tell somebody, “Yeah, I’ll follow up on that,” and then it just slips out of my head.\n\nAnd then suddenly it’s days later—or someone’s asking me about it—and I’m standing there like, oh no, I was supposed to do that. It’s happened enough now that work basically sent me here before I mess up my shot at moving up, which… yeah, that’s pretty embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing on tasks or paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… focusing is definitely part of it. Like I’ll open a digital work order thinking, okay, I’m going to handle this, I’ve got the notes right here. And then halfway through reading it, my brain just kind of… goes somewhere else. There’ll be a noise in the shop, or I’ll think about the next job, or somebody walks by, and then I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read.\n\nIt’s annoying because I’m not trying to ignore it. I’m looking right at it. I’ll have to reread the same thing, and sometimes even then I get pulled off again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Mood? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I’m looking right at it. I’ll have to reread the same thing, and sometimes even then I get pulled off again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Mood? I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any headaches or physical discomfort lately?\n\nPatient: Um… headaches? Not really that I’ve noticed. I mean, everybody gets a headache here and there, right? Especially if I skip lunch or I’m staring at something too long, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything lately that made me think, “Oh, this is a problem.”\n\nPhysical discomfort… I don’t know. My job’s physical, so I’m kind of always aware of my back or knees or whatever by the end of the day, but nothing new that I can point to. Sorry, I’m probably over-answering that. Nothing stands out lately.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.

Anxious about things coming up… um, I don’t know. Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, my workspace looks messy to some people, probably, but I usually kind of know where the important stuff is. Or I think I do.

Like I’ll open a digital work order thinking, okay, I’m going to handle this, I’ve got the notes right here. And then halfway through reading it, my brain just kind of… goes somewhere else. There’ll be a noise in the shop, or I’ll think about the next job, or somebody walks by, and then I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read.\n\nIt’s annoying because I’m not trying to ignore it. I’m looking right at it. I’ll have to reread the same thing, and sometimes even then I get pulled off again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Mood? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any headaches or physical discomfort lately?\n\nPatient: Um… headaches? Not really that I’ve noticed. I mean, everybody gets a headache here and there, right? My job’s physical, so I’m kind of always aware of my back or knees or whatever by the end of the day, but nothing new that I can point to. Sorry, I’m probably over-answering that. Nothing stands out lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.\n\nBut I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. I’m not sure… I mean, nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your memory is ruining things,” or anything like that.

I guess sometimes people get irritated if I lose the thread of what they’re saying, but then again, everybody in my family talks over each other, so it’s hard to tell what’s me and what’s just… how we are. Like my sister will start telling me something, then bring up her neighbor’s dog, and then I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill—sorry, that’s not what you asked.

So, problems in relationships… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought of it that way. I don’t think I have a clear example.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Confused or disoriented… I don’t know. Not like I don’t know where I am, if that’s what you mean.

Sometimes I’ll kind of lose track of what I was doing, but that feels different than being disoriented, I think. Like if I walk into the garage and forget why I went in there—well, everybody does that, right? My mom used to do that constantly, she’d blame it on having too much in her head, and honestly I think that’s just life sometimes.

So… confused, maybe in the sense of getting turned around in a task or conversation, but not confused like I’m lost or don’t recognize things. I’m not sure if that answers it.

Yeah… I think so. I was kind of trying not to make it sound dramatic, but at work it’s gotten pretty serious.

I’ve got a backlog now, like jobs that should’ve been closed out and aren’t, and tenants have complained. Some of them are reasonable, some of them complain if a lightbulb flickers for ten seconds—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is it’s on me now.

I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any headaches or physical discomfort lately?\n\nPatient: Um… headaches? Not really that I’ve noticed. I mean, everybody gets a headache here and there, right? Especially if I skip lunch or I’m staring at something too long, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything lately that made me think, “Oh, this is a problem.”\n\nPhysical discomfort… I don’t know. My job’s physical, so I’m kind of always aware of my back or knees or whatever by the end of the day, but nothing new that I can point to. Sorry, I’m probably over-answering that. Nothing stands out lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.\n\nBut I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. Nothing really stands out to me as unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: Anxious about things coming up… um, I don’t know. I mean, if there’s something scheduled, like a job I know is gonna be a pain, I might think about it a little, but I don’t know if I’d call that anxiety. More like trying to remember what I need to bring, and then I’ll get sidetracked because I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that part from the shop,” and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill.\n\nSo… worried, maybe sometimes, but not in a way I’ve really paid attention to. I guess I’m not sure what counts as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering how to do tasks or skills that you've learned in the past?\n\nPatient: Remembering how to do stuff I already know? Um… I don’t think so. Like if I’ve learned a repair or a routine, it usually comes back once I’m in front of it.\n\nSometimes I’ll stand there and be like, “What was I about to grab?” but that’s not really forgetting the skill, you know? That’s more like… I walk into the shop for one thing and see three other things that need doing, and then suddenly I’m looking for gloves I already had in my pocket. But the actual how-to part? I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. And then I have to reread it, sometimes more than once. It’s annoying because it’s not like I walked away from it, I’m still sitting right there, but I’m not really with it for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, my workspace looks messy to some people, probably, but I usually kind of know where the important stuff is. Or I think I do.\n\nLike at work, there’s always parts, tools, paperwork, random things that someone set down “just for a minute,” and then three days later nobody knows whose it was. But that’s kind of normal in maintenance, too, so I don’t know if that counts as me being disorganized or just the job being that way.\n\nTasks… I try to keep track, but I guess it depends what’s going on. Sometimes I’ll start making a list and then the list ends up on the passenger seat under a receipt or something. Sorry, I’m probably not answering right. What do you mean by organizing, like keeping things neat, or knowing what order to do jobs in?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships because of your memory or focus issues?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your memory is ruining things,” or anything like that.\n\nI guess sometimes people get irritated if I lose the thread of what they’re saying, but then again, everybody in my family talks over each other, so it’s hard to tell what’s me and what’s just… how we are. Like my sister will start telling me something, then bring up her neighbor’s dog, and then I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nSo, problems in relationships… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought of it that way. I don’t think I have a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented?\n\nPatient: Confused or disoriented… I don’t know. Not like I don’t know where I am, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of lose track of what I was doing, but that feels different than being disoriented, I think. Like if I walk into the garage and forget why I went in there—well, everybody does that, right? My mom used to do that constantly, she’d blame it on having too much in her head, and honestly I think that’s just life sometimes.\n\nSo… confused, maybe in the sense of getting turned around in a task or conversation, but not confused like I’m lost or don’t recognize things. I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory or focus issues caused you any significant problems at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I was kind of trying not to make it sound dramatic, but at work it’s gotten pretty serious.\n\nI’ve got a backlog now, like jobs that should’ve been closed out and aren’t, and tenants have complained. Some of them are reasonable, some of them complain if a lightbulb flickers for ten seconds—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is it’s on me now.\n\nAnd there was a safety review put on my record, which… I hate even saying that out loud. I’ve worked hard to be seen as someone reliable, and I was hoping to keep moving toward a promotion path, maybe lead or supervisor eventually. Now I’m scared this is going to follow me and cost me that. So yes, it’s causing real problems.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s gotten worse, I think. Not forever, exactly… more like the last year or so.

Actually, probably about 11 months, because that’s when the new management company took over and changed everything to digital work orders. Before that, I could keep track better with the old system, or at least I had my way of doing it. Now there’s all this stricter documentation, clicking through screens, updating statuses—sorry, I’m getting off track.

But yes, worse. Noticeably worse since that change at work.

Like I’ll open a digital work order thinking, okay, I’m going to handle this, I’ve got the notes right here. And then halfway through reading it, my brain just kind of… goes somewhere else. There’ll be a noise in the shop, or I’ll think about the next job, or somebody walks by, and then I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read.\n\nIt’s annoying because I’m not trying to ignore it. I’m looking right at it. I’ll have to reread the same thing, and sometimes even then I get pulled off again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Mood? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any headaches or physical discomfort lately?\n\nPatient: Um… headaches? Not really that I’ve noticed. I mean, everybody gets a headache here and there, right? Especially if I skip lunch or I’m staring at something too long, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything lately that made me think, “Oh, this is a problem.”\n\nPhysical discomfort… I don’t know. My job’s physical, so I’m kind of always aware of my back or knees or whatever by the end of the day, but nothing new that I can point to. Sorry, I’m probably over-answering that. Nothing stands out lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.\n\nBut I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. Nothing really stands out to me as unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: Anxious about things coming up… um, I don’t know. I mean, if there’s something scheduled, like a job I know is gonna be a pain, I might think about it a little, but I don’t know if I’d call that anxiety. More like trying to remember what I need to bring, and then I’ll get sidetracked because I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that part from the shop,” and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill.\n\nSo… worried, maybe sometimes, but not in a way I’ve really paid attention to. I guess I’m not sure what counts as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering how to do tasks or skills that you've learned in the past?\n\nPatient: Remembering how to do stuff I already know? Um… I don’t think so. Like if I’ve learned a repair or a routine, it usually comes back once I’m in front of it.\n\nSometimes I’ll stand there and be like, “What was I about to grab?” but that’s not really forgetting the skill, you know? That’s more like… I walk into the shop for one thing and see three other things that need doing, and then suddenly I’m looking for gloves I already had in my pocket. But the actual how-to part? I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. And then I have to reread it, sometimes more than once. It’s annoying because it’s not like I walked away from it, I’m still sitting right there, but I’m not really with it for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, my workspace looks messy to some people, probably, but I usually kind of know where the important stuff is. Or I think I do.\n\nLike at work, there’s always parts, tools, paperwork, random things that someone set down “just for a minute,” and then three days later nobody knows whose it was. But that’s kind of normal in maintenance, too, so I don’t know if that counts as me being disorganized or just the job being that way.\n\nTasks… I try to keep track, but I guess it depends what’s going on. Sometimes I’ll start making a list and then the list ends up on the passenger seat under a receipt or something. Sorry, I’m probably not answering right. Like my sister will start telling me something, then bring up her neighbor’s dog, and then I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nSo, problems in relationships… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought of it that way. I don’t think I have a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented?\n\nPatient: Confused or disoriented… I don’t know. Not like I don’t know where I am, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of lose track of what I was doing, but that feels different than being disoriented, I think. Like if I walk into the garage and forget why I went in there—well, everybody does that, right? My mom used to do that constantly, she’d blame it on having too much in her head, and honestly I think that’s just life sometimes.\n\nSo… confused, maybe in the sense of getting turned around in a task or conversation, but not confused like I’m lost or don’t recognize things. I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory or focus issues caused you any significant problems at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I was kind of trying not to make it sound dramatic, but at work it’s gotten pretty serious.\n\nI’ve got a backlog now, like jobs that should’ve been closed out and aren’t, and tenants have complained. I just keep losing track of things I swore I’d remember.\n\nDoctor: Are you also forgetting things outside of work, like appointments or tasks you need to do at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the big thing, the thing that got me sent here, is more at work. Like I’ll open a digital work order thinking, okay, I’m going to handle this, I’ve got the notes right here. And then halfway through reading it, my brain just kind of… goes somewhere else. There’ll be a noise in the shop, or I’ll think about the next job, or somebody walks by, and then I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read.\n\nIt’s annoying because I’m not trying to ignore it. I’m looking right at it. I’ll have to reread the same thing, and sometimes even then I get pulled off again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Mood? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. Nothing stands out lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.\n\nBut I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. I mean, if there’s something scheduled, like a job I know is gonna be a pain, I might think about it a little, but I don’t know if I’d call that anxiety. More like trying to remember what I need to bring, and then I’ll get sidetracked because I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that part from the shop,” and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill.\n\nSo… worried, maybe sometimes, but not in a way I’ve really paid attention to. I guess I’m not sure what counts as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering how to do tasks or skills that you've learned in the past?\n\nPatient: Remembering how to do stuff I already know? Um… I don’t think so. Like if I’ve learned a repair or a routine, it usually comes back once I’m in front of it.\n\nSometimes I’ll stand there and be like, “What was I about to grab?” but that’s not really forgetting the skill, you know? That’s more like… I walk into the shop for one thing and see three other things that need doing, and then suddenly I’m looking for gloves I already had in my pocket. But the actual how-to part? I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. And then I have to reread it, sometimes more than once. It’s annoying because it’s not like I walked away from it, I’m still sitting right there, but I’m not really with it for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. But that’s kind of normal in maintenance, too, so I don’t know if that counts as me being disorganized or just the job being that way.\n\nTasks… I try to keep track, but I guess it depends what’s going on. Sometimes I’ll start making a list and then the list ends up on the passenger seat under a receipt or something. Sorry, I’m probably not answering right. I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any headaches or physical discomfort lately?\n\nPatient: Um… headaches? Not really that I’ve noticed. I mean, everybody gets a headache here and there, right? Especially if I skip lunch or I’m staring at something too long, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything lately that made me think, “Oh, this is a problem.”\n\nPhysical discomfort… I don’t know. My job’s physical, so I’m kind of always aware of my back or knees or whatever by the end of the day, but nothing new that I can point to. Sorry, I’m probably over-answering that. Nothing stands out lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.\n\nBut I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. Nothing really stands out to me as unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: Anxious about things coming up… um, I don’t know. I mean, if there’s something scheduled, like a job I know is gonna be a pain, I might think about it a little, but I don’t know if I’d call that anxiety. More like trying to remember what I need to bring, and then I’ll get sidetracked because I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that part from the shop,” and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill.\n\nSo… worried, maybe sometimes, but not in a way I’ve really paid attention to. I guess I’m not sure what counts as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering how to do tasks or skills that you've learned in the past?\n\nPatient: Remembering how to do stuff I already know? Um… I don’t think so. Like if I’ve learned a repair or a routine, it usually comes back once I’m in front of it.\n\nSometimes I’ll stand there and be like, “What was I about to grab?” but that’s not really forgetting the skill, you know? That’s more like… I walk into the shop for one thing and see three other things that need doing, and then suddenly I’m looking for gloves I already had in my pocket. But the actual how-to part? I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. And then I have to reread it, sometimes more than once. It’s annoying because it’s not like I walked away from it, I’m still sitting right there, but I’m not really with it for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, my workspace looks messy to some people, probably, but I usually kind of know where the important stuff is. Or I think I do.\n\nLike at work, there’s always parts, tools, paperwork, random things that someone set down “just for a minute,” and then three days later nobody knows whose it was. But that’s kind of normal in maintenance, too, so I don’t know if that counts as me being disorganized or just the job being that way.\n\nTasks… I try to keep track, but I guess it depends what’s going on. Sometimes I’ll start making a list and then the list ends up on the passenger seat under a receipt or something. Sorry, I’m probably not answering right. What do you mean by organizing, like keeping things neat, or knowing what order to do jobs in?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships because of your memory or focus issues?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your memory is ruining things,” or anything like that.\n\nI guess sometimes people get irritated if I lose the thread of what they’re saying, but then again, everybody in my family talks over each other, so it’s hard to tell what’s me and what’s just… how we are. Like my sister will start telling me something, then bring up her neighbor’s dog, and then I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nSo, problems in relationships… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought of it that way. I don’t think I have a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented?\n\nPatient: Confused or disoriented… I don’t know. Not like I don’t know where I am, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of lose track of what I was doing, but that feels different than being disoriented, I think. Like if I walk into the garage and forget why I went in there—well, everybody does that, right? My mom used to do that constantly, she’d blame it on having too much in her head, and honestly I think that’s just life sometimes.\n\nSo… confused, maybe in the sense of getting turned around in a task or conversation, but not confused like I’m lost or don’t recognize things. I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory or focus issues caused you any significant problems at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I was kind of trying not to make it sound dramatic, but at work it’s gotten pretty serious.\n\nI’ve got a backlog now, like jobs that should’ve been closed out and aren’t, and tenants have complained. Some of them are reasonable, some of them complain if a lightbulb flickers for ten seconds—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is it’s on me now.\n\nAnd there was a safety review put on my record, which… I hate even saying that out loud. I’ve worked hard to be seen as someone reliable, and I was hoping to keep moving toward a promotion path, maybe lead or supervisor eventually. Now I’m scared this is going to follow me and cost me that. So yes, it’s causing real problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these memory problems getting better, worse, or staying about the same over time?\n\nPatient: It’s gotten worse, I think. Not forever, exactly… more like the last year or so.\n\nActually, probably about 11 months, because that’s when the new management company took over and changed everything to digital work orders. Before that, I could keep track better with the old system, or at least I had my way of doing it. Now there’s all this stricter documentation, clicking through screens, updating statuses—sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yes, worse. Noticeably worse since that change at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stress or traumatic events in the last year, around the time your memory problems started getting worse?\n\nPatient: Um… significant stress or trauma? I don’t think I’d call anything traumatic, no.\n\nStress, yeah, but that was more the work changes I mentioned—the new company and the digital stuff and all the documentation. I mean, everybody was stressed about it, not just me. There wasn’t like… a big event, or an accident, or anything personal that happened around then that I can point to.\n\nUnless you mean work stress counts, because then yes, but nothing like trauma.\n\nDoctor: Did the memory problems start exactly when the new digital work order system was introduced at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say exactly the same day, like a switch flipped or anything. It was more… once the new management company came in and everything moved over to the digital work orders, that’s when I started noticing it getting worse.\n\nSo, yeah, around then. About 11 months ago. The stricter documentation and having to keep up with all those updates is when it became a real problem, or at least a lot harder to hide.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah, I think so… I mean, maybe. When I’m left alone with one thing, it’s not quite as bad.

But even then, like with the digital work orders, I’ll open one up thinking, okay, I’ve got this, just read the notes and do the next step. And then halfway through, there’s a noise, or I remember another job, or someone walks by, and my brain just kind of… slides off. Then I’m staring at the screen and I realize I don’t actually know what I just read.

It’s embarrassing, because it’s right there in front of me. I’ll have to go back and reread it, and then sometimes I get annoyed with myself, which probably doesn’t help. So yeah, fewer interruptions would help, but it’s like I still have to fight to stay on the one thing.

Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. Nothing really stands out to me as unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: Anxious about things coming up… um, I don’t know. I mean, if there’s something scheduled, like a job I know is gonna be a pain, I might think about it a little, but I don’t know if I’d call that anxiety. More like trying to remember what I need to bring, and then I’ll get sidetracked because I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that part from the shop,” and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill.\n\nSo… worried, maybe sometimes, but not in a way I’ve really paid attention to. I guess I’m not sure what counts as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering how to do tasks or skills that you've learned in the past?\n\nPatient: Remembering how to do stuff I already know? Um… I don’t think so. Like if I’ve learned a repair or a routine, it usually comes back once I’m in front of it.\n\nSometimes I’ll stand there and be like, “What was I about to grab?” but that’s not really forgetting the skill, you know? That’s more like… I walk into the shop for one thing and see three other things that need doing, and then suddenly I’m looking for gloves I already had in my pocket. But the actual how-to part? I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. And then I have to reread it, sometimes more than once. It’s annoying because it’s not like I walked away from it, I’m still sitting right there, but I’m not really with it for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, my workspace looks messy to some people, probably, but I usually kind of know where the important stuff is. Or I think I do.\n\nLike at work, there’s always parts, tools, paperwork, random things that someone set down “just for a minute,” and then three days later nobody knows whose it was. But that’s kind of normal in maintenance, too, so I don’t know if that counts as me being disorganized or just the job being that way.\n\nTasks… I try to keep track, but I guess it depends what’s going on. Sometimes I’ll start making a list and then the list ends up on the passenger seat under a receipt or something. Sorry, I’m probably not answering right. What do you mean by organizing, like keeping things neat, or knowing what order to do jobs in?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships because of your memory or focus issues?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your memory is ruining things,” or anything like that.\n\nI guess sometimes people get irritated if I lose the thread of what they’re saying, but then again, everybody in my family talks over each other, so it’s hard to tell what’s me and what’s just… how we are. Like my sister will start telling me something, then bring up her neighbor’s dog, and then I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nSo, problems in relationships… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought of it that way. I don’t think I have a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented?\n\nPatient: Confused or disoriented… I don’t know. Not like I don’t know where I am, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of lose track of what I was doing, but that feels different than being disoriented, I think. Like if I walk into the garage and forget why I went in there—well, everybody does that, right? My mom used to do that constantly, she’d blame it on having too much in her head, and honestly I think that’s just life sometimes.\n\nSo… confused, maybe in the sense of getting turned around in a task or conversation, but not confused like I’m lost or don’t recognize things. I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory or focus issues caused you any significant problems at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I was kind of trying not to make it sound dramatic, but at work it’s gotten pretty serious.\n\nI’ve got a backlog now, like jobs that should’ve been closed out and aren’t, and tenants have complained. Some of them are reasonable, some of them complain if a lightbulb flickers for ten seconds—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is it’s on me now.\n\nAnd there was a safety review put on my record, which… I hate even saying that out loud. I’ve worked hard to be seen as someone reliable, and I was hoping to keep moving toward a promotion path, maybe lead or supervisor eventually. Now I’m scared this is going to follow me and cost me that. So yes, it’s causing real problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these memory problems getting better, worse, or staying about the same over time?\n\nPatient: It’s gotten worse, I think. I mean, everybody was stressed about it, not just me. There wasn’t like… a big event, or an accident, or anything personal that happened around then that I can point to.\n\nUnless you mean work stress counts, because then yes, but nothing like trauma.\n\nDoctor: Did the memory problems start exactly when the new digital work order system was introduced at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say exactly the same day, like a switch flipped or anything. It was more… once the new management company came in and everything moved over to the digital work orders, that’s when I started noticing it getting worse.\n\nSo, yeah, around then. About 11 months ago. The stricter documentation and having to keep up with all those updates is when it became a real problem, or at least a lot harder to hide.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble remembering things you need to do at home, like taking medication or paying bills?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, yeah, I forget stuff I’m supposed to do, but the bigger mess has been work. And then I’ll remember later, like way too late, and by then it’s already caused a problem.\n\nIt’s gotten piled up enough that they said I need to get it checked out before I mess up my chance to move up. I just keep losing track of things I swore I’d remember.\n\nDoctor: Are you also forgetting things outside of work, like appointments or tasks you need to do at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… the big thing, the thing that got me sent here, is more at work. I keep forgetting scheduled callbacks and work orders, and even just basic follow-ups I told people I’d take care of. Like I’ll promise someone, “Yeah, I’ve got it,” and then it just… disappears on me until it’s already become a problem.\n\nAt home, I don’t know, it’s harder to separate out because everybody forgets stuff, right? Like you set something down and then—anyway. But what really piled up and got serious is the work stuff. They basically told me I need to get checked out before I ruin my chance at moving up.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble remembering things like birthdays, anniversaries, or personal appointments outside of work?\n\nPatient: I mean… birthdays and anniversaries, I’m not really sure that’s the main thing. I have calendars and people remind me, you know? What actually brought me in is more like at work, where I’ll have a scheduled callback or a work order, or I’ll tell somebody, “Yeah, I’ll follow up on that,” and then it just slips out of my head.\n\nAnd then suddenly it’s days later—or someone’s asking me about it—and I’m standing there like, oh no, I was supposed to do that. It’s happened enough now that work basically sent me here before I mess up my shot at moving up, which… yeah, that’s pretty embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing on tasks or paying attention to what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… focusing is definitely part of it. Like I’ll open a digital work order thinking, okay, I’m going to handle this, I’ve got the notes right here. And then halfway through reading it, my brain just kind of… goes somewhere else. There’ll be a noise in the shop, or I’ll think about the next job, or somebody walks by, and then I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read.\n\nIt’s annoying because I’m not trying to ignore it. I’m looking right at it. I’ll have to reread the same thing, and sometimes even then I get pulled off again.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Mood? Um… I don’t know. I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I get frustrated when stuff piles up, obviously, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my mood.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be fine, and then something at work comes up and I’m annoyed at myself, but that feels kind of normal given the situation. I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any headaches or physical discomfort lately?\n\nPatient: Um… headaches? Not really that I’ve noticed. I mean, everybody gets a headache here and there, right? Especially if I skip lunch or I’m staring at something too long, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything lately that made me think, “Oh, this is a problem.”\n\nPhysical discomfort… I don’t know. My job’s physical, so I’m kind of always aware of my back or knees or whatever by the end of the day, but nothing new that I can point to. Sorry, I’m probably over-answering that. Nothing stands out lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Tired… I mean, I guess everybody’s tired, right? I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed something specific like, “Wow, I have no energy.” My work can wear me out, especially depending on the job, and then by the time I get home I’m not exactly bouncing around.\n\nBut I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sure I can separate being tired from just… having a full day, if that makes sense. Nothing really stands out to me as unusual.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: Anxious about things coming up… um, I don’t know. I mean, if there’s something scheduled, like a job I know is gonna be a pain, I might think about it a little, but I don’t know if I’d call that anxiety. More like trying to remember what I need to bring, and then I’ll get sidetracked because I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that part from the shop,” and then somehow I’m thinking about whether I paid the electric bill.\n\nSo… worried, maybe sometimes, but not in a way I’ve really paid attention to. I guess I’m not sure what counts as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering how to do tasks or skills that you've learned in the past?\n\nPatient: Remembering how to do stuff I already know? Um… I don’t think so. Like if I’ve learned a repair or a routine, it usually comes back once I’m in front of it.\n\nSometimes I’ll stand there and be like, “What was I about to grab?” but that’s not really forgetting the skill, you know? That’s more like… I walk into the shop for one thing and see three other things that need doing, and then suddenly I’m looking for gloves I already had in my pocket. But the actual how-to part? I haven’t really noticed losing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself forgetting what you were doing in the middle of a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one happens. Like I’ll be in the middle of putting in a digital work order, and I think I’m following it fine, reading the notes or whatever, and then there’s a noise, or somebody mentions another job, and my brain just kind of… goes over there.\n\nThen I look back at the screen and realize I don’t actually know what I just read, or what part I was on. And then I have to reread it, sometimes more than once. It’s annoying because it’s not like I walked away from it, I’m still sitting right there, but I’m not really with it for a minute.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or workspace?\n\nPatient: Organizing my tasks or workspace… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I mean, my workspace looks messy to some people, probably, but I usually kind of know where the important stuff is. Or I think I do.\n\nLike at work, there’s always parts, tools, paperwork, random things that someone set down “just for a minute,” and then three days later nobody knows whose it was. But that’s kind of normal in maintenance, too, so I don’t know if that counts as me being disorganized or just the job being that way.\n\nTasks… I try to keep track, but I guess it depends what’s going on. Sometimes I’ll start making a list and then the list ends up on the passenger seat under a receipt or something. Sorry, I’m probably not answering right. What do you mean by organizing, like keeping things neat, or knowing what order to do jobs in?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships because of your memory or focus issues?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your memory is ruining things,” or anything like that.\n\nI guess sometimes people get irritated if I lose the thread of what they’re saying, but then again, everybody in my family talks over each other, so it’s hard to tell what’s me and what’s just… how we are. Like my sister will start telling me something, then bring up her neighbor’s dog, and then I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nSo, problems in relationships… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought of it that way. I don’t think I have a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused or disoriented?\n\nPatient: Confused or disoriented… I don’t know. Not like I don’t know where I am, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll kind of lose track of what I was doing, but that feels different than being disoriented, I think. Like if I walk into the garage and forget why I went in there—well, everybody does that, right? My mom used to do that constantly, she’d blame it on having too much in her head, and honestly I think that’s just life sometimes.\n\nSo… confused, maybe in the sense of getting turned around in a task or conversation, but not confused like I’m lost or don’t recognize things. I’m not sure if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory or focus issues caused you any significant problems at work or in your personal life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I was kind of trying not to make it sound dramatic, but at work it’s gotten pretty serious.\n\nI’ve got a backlog now, like jobs that should’ve been closed out and aren’t, and tenants have complained. Some of them are reasonable, some of them complain if a lightbulb flickers for ten seconds—sorry, that’s not the point. The point is it’s on me now.\n\nAnd there was a safety review put on my record, which… I hate even saying that out loud. I’ve worked hard to be seen as someone reliable, and I was hoping to keep moving toward a promotion path, maybe lead or supervisor eventually. Now I’m scared this is going to follow me and cost me that. So yes, it’s causing real problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these memory problems getting better, worse, or staying about the same over time?\n\nPatient: It’s gotten worse, I think. Not forever, exactly… more like the last year or so.\n\nActually, probably about 11 months, because that’s when the new management company took over and changed everything to digital work orders. Before that, I could keep track better with the old system, or at least I had my way of doing it. Now there’s all this stricter documentation, clicking through screens, updating statuses—sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yes, worse. Noticeably worse since that change at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stress or traumatic events in the last year, around the time your memory problems started getting worse?\n\nPatient: Um… significant stress or trauma? I don’t think I’d call anything traumatic, no.\n\nStress, yeah, but that was more the work changes I mentioned—the new company and the digital stuff and all the documentation. I mean, everybody was stressed about it, not just me. There wasn’t like… a big event, or an accident, or anything personal that happened around then that I can point to.\n\nUnless you mean work stress counts, because then yes, but nothing like trauma.\n\nDoctor: Did the memory problems start exactly when the new digital work order system was introduced at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say exactly the same day, like a switch flipped or anything. It was more… once the new management company came in and everything moved over to the digital work orders, that’s when I started noticing it getting worse.\n\nSo, yeah, around then. About 11 months ago. The stricter documentation and having to keep up with all those updates is when it became a real problem, or at least a lot harder to hide.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble remembering things you need to do at home, like taking medication or paying bills?\n\nPatient: At home… I mean, yeah, I forget stuff I’m supposed to do, but the bigger mess has been work. That’s really what brought me in.\n\nI keep missing scheduled callbacks and forgetting work orders, or I’ll tell someone, “Yeah, I’ll follow up on that,” and then it just… drops out unless it’s right in front of me. And then later there’s three people waiting and it looks like I just didn’t care, which isn’t true.\n\nIt’s piled up enough that work basically sent me here, because I’m trying not to blow my chance at moving up. I know you asked about home stuff—bills and medicine and all that—but the work follow-ups are the ones getting me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would remember things better if you could focus on one task at a time without interruptions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so… I mean, maybe. When I’m left alone with one thing, it’s not quite as bad.\n\nBut even then, like with the digital work orders, I’ll open one up thinking, okay, I’ve got this, just read the notes and do the next step. And then halfway through, there’s a noise, or I remember another job, or someone walks by, and my brain just kind of… slides off. Then I’m staring at the screen and I realize I don’t actually know what I just read.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, because it’s right there in front of me. I’ll have to go back and reread it, and then sometimes I get annoyed with myself, which probably doesn’t help. So yeah, fewer interruptions would help, but it’s like I still have to fight to stay on the one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your main problem is forgetting things or having trouble paying attention to them in the first place?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the paying attention part, honestly. Like I’ll start a digital work order thinking, okay, I’ve got this, I’m reading the notes, I know what I’m supposed to do… and then halfway through, my mind just slips off.\n\nSometimes it’s a noise, sometimes I think of another job I’m supposed to get to, and then I look back and realize I don’t know what I just looked at. So then later it seems like I forgot it, but maybe I never really took it in right in the first place."
